In the aftermath, he helped organize vigils and spoke out against gun violence, but privately he found that every trip past that corner and every late-night siren pulled him back into the terror of that night, even as he tried to maintain his role as a steady spiritual leader. Over time, his difficulty staying present during services, planning youth activities in that neighborhood, and riding the subway after dark began to interfere with both seminary work and church responsibilities, forcing him to cancel commitments and lean on others to cover for him. When an older pastor gently confronted him about his growing avoidance of certain ministry tasks and his visible tension during community events, Lacedrick agreed to seek psychiatric help, saying he could no longer keep functioning in his calling while feeling constantly on edge.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to love running open gym nights and playing ball with the kids, but now even when I’m there in the gym I just kind of go through the motions and can’t feel any real joy or excitement about it anymore, so I make excuses to let other leaders take over instead of me. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because I just can’t hold a thought long enough to finish anything. \n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere, especially if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I’m right back on that sidewalk with that boy bleeding in front of me, hearing the shots and feeling the concrete under my face like it’s happening again, and it plays over in my head on a loop no matter how much I pray or try to distract myself. \n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I plan my whole day around not having to walk past that corner or be outside when it’s getting dark, and if a church event means I’d have to be on that block or even talk about the shooting, I suddenly find a reason not to go or change the subject so I don’t have to feel everything come rushing back. \n- S073 (Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame): I keep telling myself that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like I’m failing in my calling.\n- duration: These problems have been going on ever since the shooting about a little over two years ago and really haven’t let up.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

In the aftermath, he helped organize vigils and spoke out against gun violence, but privately he found that every trip past that corner and every late-night siren pulled him back into the terror of that night, even as he tried to maintain his role as a steady spiritual leader. Over time, his difficulty staying present during services, planning youth activities in that neighborhood, and riding the subway after dark began to interfere with both seminary work and church responsibilities, forcing him to cancel commitments and lean on others to cover for him. When an older pastor gently confronted him about his growing avoidance of certain ministry tasks and his visible tension during community events, Lacedrick agreed to seek psychiatric help, saying he could no longer keep functioning in his calling while feeling constantly on edge.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to love running open gym nights and playing ball with the kids, but now even when I’m there in the gym I just kind of go through the motions and can’t feel any real joy or excitement about it anymore, so I make excuses to let other leaders take over instead of me. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because I just can’t hold a thought long enough to finish anything. \n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere, especially if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I’m right back on that sidewalk with that boy bleeding in front of me, hearing the shots and feeling the concrete under my face like it’s happening again, and it plays over in my head on a loop no matter how much I pray or try to distract myself. \n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I plan my whole day around not having to walk past that corner or be outside when it’s getting dark, and if a church event means I’d have to be on that block or even talk about the shooting, I suddenly find a reason not to go or change the subject so I don’t have to feel everything come rushing back. \n- S073 (Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame): I keep telling myself that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like I’m failing in my calling.\n- duration: These problems have been going on ever since the shooting about a little over two years ago and really haven’t let up.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can It has gotten to the point where I just can't keep functioning in my daily life or my ministry while feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, or feeling like you might die or lose control?\n\nPatient: I don't really experience sudden, overwhelming panic attacks that peak out of nowhere like that. In the aftermath, he helped organize vigils and spoke out against gun violence, but privately he found that every trip past that corner and every late-night siren pulled him back into the terror of that night, even as he tried to maintain his role as a steady spiritual leader. Over time, his difficulty staying present during services, planning youth activities in that neighborhood, and riding the subway after dark began to interfere with both seminary work and church responsibilities, forcing him to cancel commitments and lean on others to cover for him. When an older pastor gently confronted him about his growing avoidance of certain ministry tasks and his visible tension during community events, Lacedrick agreed to seek psychiatric help, saying he could no longer keep functioning in his calling while feeling constantly on edge.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to love running open gym nights and playing ball with the kids, but now even when I’m there in the gym I just kind of go through the motions and can’t feel any real joy or excitement about it anymore, so I make excuses to let other leaders take over instead of me. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because I just can’t hold a thought long enough to finish anything. \n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere, especially if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I’m right back on that sidewalk with that boy bleeding in front of me, hearing the shots and feeling the concrete under my face like it’s happening again, and it plays over in my head on a loop no matter how much I pray or try to distract myself. \n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I plan my whole day around not having to walk past that corner or be outside when it’s getting dark, and if a church event means I’d have to be on that block or even talk about the shooting, I suddenly find a reason not to go or change the subject so I don’t have to feel everything come rushing back. \n- S073 (Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame): I keep telling myself that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like I’m failing in my calling.\n- duration: These problems have been going on ever since the shooting about a little over two years ago and really haven’t let up.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can It has been going on constantly since that night, so it has actually been much longer than six months.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, do you have unwanted memories, nightmares, flashbacks, or strong distress when something reminds you of it, and do you avoid places or situations that bring it to mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I'm instantly right back on that sidewalk seeing that boy bleed and feeling the concrete under my face. To keep that from happening, I plan my whole day to avoid walking past that corner or being out after dark, and I try to avoid talking about the shooting at all.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you also had trouble sleeping, being easily startled, irritability, trouble concentrating, or feeling emotionally numb or detached from other people?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel like there's a glass wall separating me from everyone else, and I've been snapping at people over minor things, which makes me feel terrible afterward. My mind also goes blank when I try to read or prepare sermons, and I feel completely numb, unable to find any joy in things I used to love like running our youth gym nights.\n\nDoctor: When you’re reminded of the shooting, do you ever feel like you are reliving it, or like the world around you is unreal or you are somehow outside your body?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely feel like I'm reliving it; when a car backfires, I'm instantly back on that sidewalk with the boy bleeding in front of me and the concrete under my face. I don't feel like I'm literally outside of my body, but it does feel like there's a glass wall between me and everyone else, as if they are in a completely different world and I'm just pretending to be there."

In the aftermath, he helped organize vigils and spoke out against gun violence, but privately he found that every trip past that corner and every late-night siren pulled him back into the terror of that night, even as he tried to maintain his role as a steady spiritual leader. Over time, his difficulty staying present during services, planning youth activities in that neighborhood, and riding the subway after dark began to interfere with both seminary work and church responsibilities, forcing him to cancel commitments and lean on others to cover for him. When an older pastor gently confronted him about his growing avoidance of certain ministry tasks and his visible tension during community events, Lacedrick agreed to seek psychiatric help, saying he could no longer keep functioning in his calling while feeling constantly on edge.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to love running open gym nights and playing ball with the kids, but now even when I’m there in the gym I just kind of go through the motions and can’t feel any real joy or excitement about it anymore, so I make excuses to let other leaders take over instead of me. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because I just can’t hold a thought long enough to finish anything. \n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere, especially if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I’m right back on that sidewalk with that boy bleeding in front of me, hearing the shots and feeling the concrete under my face like it’s happening again, and it plays over in my head on a loop no matter how much I pray or try to distract myself. \n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I plan my whole day around not having to walk past that corner or be outside when it’s getting dark, and if a church event means I’d have to be on that block or even talk about the shooting, I suddenly find a reason not to go or change the subject so I don’t have to feel everything come rushing back. \n- S073 (Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame): I keep telling myself that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like I’m failing in my calling.\n- duration: These problems have been going on ever since the shooting about a little over two years ago and really haven’t let up.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can When an older pastor gently confronted him about his growing avoidance of certain ministry tasks and his visible tension during community events, Lacedrick agreed to seek psychiatric help, saying he could no longer keep functioning in his calling while feeling constantly on edge.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to love running open gym nights and playing ball with the kids, but now even when I’m there in the gym I just kind of go through the motions and can’t feel any real joy or excitement about it anymore, so I make excuses to let other leaders take over instead of me. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because I just can’t hold a thought long enough to finish anything. \n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere, especially if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I’m right back on that sidewalk with that boy bleeding in front of me, hearing the shots and feeling the concrete under my face like it’s happening again, and it plays over in my head on a loop no matter how much I pray or try to distract myself. \n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I plan my whole day around not having to walk past that corner or be outside when it’s getting dark, and if a church event means I’d have to be on that block or even talk about the shooting, I suddenly find a reason not to go or change the subject so I don’t have to feel everything come rushing back. \n- S073 (Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame): I keep telling myself that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like I’m failing in my calling.\n- duration: These problems have been going on ever since the shooting about a little over two years ago and really haven’t let up.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m Dr. ____. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. I'm here because I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood. It has gotten to the point where I just can't keep functioning in my daily life or my ministry while feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, or feeling like you might die or lose control?\n\nPatient: I don't really experience sudden, overwhelming panic attacks that peak out of nowhere like that. If so, did your symptoms start after that event and last for more than one month?\n\nPatient: Yes, about two years and three months ago, a teenage boy was shot right in front of me during a drive-by shooting, and I dropped to the ground believing I was going to be next. My symptoms started immediately after that night and have been going on constantly for over two years now.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, do you find yourself avoiding anything that reminds you of it, and do you feel more jumpy or easily startled than before?"

(Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like In the aftermath, he helped organize vigils and spoke out against gun violence, but privately he found that every trip past that corner and every late-night siren pulled him back into the terror of that night, even as he tried to maintain his role as a steady spiritual leader. Over time, his difficulty staying present during services, planning youth activities in that neighborhood, and riding the subway after dark began to interfere with both seminary work and church responsibilities, forcing him to cancel commitments and lean on others to cover for him. When an older pastor gently confronted him about his growing avoidance of certain ministry tasks and his visible tension during community events, Lacedrick agreed to seek psychiatric help, saying he could no longer keep functioning in his calling while feeling constantly on edge.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to love running open gym nights and playing ball with the kids, but now even when I’m there in the gym I just kind of go through the motions and can’t feel any real joy or excitement about it anymore, so I make excuses to let other leaders take over instead of me. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because I just can’t hold a thought long enough to finish anything. \n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere, especially if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I’m right back on that sidewalk with that boy bleeding in front of me, hearing the shots and feeling the concrete under my face like it’s happening again, and it plays over in my head on a loop no matter how much I pray or try to distract myself. \n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I plan my whole day around not having to walk past that corner or be outside when it’s getting dark, and if a church event means I’d have to be on that block or even talk about the shooting, I suddenly find a reason not to go or change the subject so I don’t have to feel everything come rushing back. \n- S073 (Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame): I keep telling myself that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like I’m failing in my calling.\n- duration: These problems have been going on ever since the shooting about a little over two years ago and really haven’t let up.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior)\nAnswer strategy: Acknowledge that you do not have a specific plan to kill yourself, but admit to engaging in reckless, life-threatening behavior. Explain that on nights when the memories are overwhelming, you drive way too fast down the highway or through the city, feeling like you don't care what happens to you in those moments. Keep a tone of honest vulnerability and distress."

    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m Dr. ____. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. I'm here because I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood. It has gotten to the point where I just can't keep functioning in my daily life or my ministry while feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, or feeling like you might die or lose control?\n\nPatient: I don't really experience sudden, overwhelming panic attacks that peak out of nowhere like that. Instead, it's more of a constant, heavy state of tension and fear, where I'm always on guard whenever I'm out. It's just a continuous, lingering feeling rather than something that comes on in sudden bursts.\n\nDoctor: Did this constant tension and fear start after a specific stressful event or major life change, and has it been going on for less than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes, it all started after a drive-by shooting I witnessed right on my block about two years and three months ago. It has been going on constantly since that night, so it has actually been much longer than six months.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, do you have unwanted memories, nightmares, flashbacks, or strong distress when something reminds you of it, and do you avoid places or situations that bring it to mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I'm instantly right back on that sidewalk seeing that boy bleed and feeling the concrete under my face. To keep that from happening, I plan my whole day to avoid walking past that corner or being out after dark, and I try to avoid talking about the shooting at all.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you also had trouble sleeping, being easily startled, irritability, trouble concentrating, or feeling emotionally numb or detached from other people?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel like there's a glass wall separating me from everyone else, and I've been snapping at people over minor things, which makes me feel terrible afterward. My mind also goes blank when I try to read or prepare sermons, and I feel completely numb, unable to find any joy in things I used to love like running our youth gym nights.\n\nDoctor: When you’re reminded of the shooting, do you ever feel like you are reliving it, or like the world around you is unreal or you are somehow outside your body?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely feel like I'm reliving it; when a car backfires, I'm instantly back on that sidewalk with the boy bleeding in front of me and the concrete under my face. I don't feel like I'm literally outside of my body, but it does feel like there's a glass wall between me and everyone else, as if they are in a completely different world and I'm just pretending to be there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a traumatic event where you were directly involved, saw someone seriously injured or killed, or believed your life was in danger? If so, did your symptoms start after that event and last for more than one month?\n\nPatient: Yes, about two years and three months ago, a teenage boy was shot right in front of me during a drive-by shooting, and I dropped to the ground believing I was going to be next. My symptoms started immediately after that night and have been going on constantly for over two years now.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, do you find yourself avoiding anything that reminds you of it, and do you feel more jumpy or easily startled than before?\n\nPatient: Yes, I plan my entire day to avoid walking past that specific corner or being outside after dark, and I'll even avoid church events if they are on that block. I'm also incredibly tense and jumpy; when I am out, I'm constantly scanning parked cars, rooftops, and people's hands, completely unable to relax.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you had any thoughts that you would be better off dead, or felt intense guilt or blame about what happened?\n\nPatient: I feel intense guilt every day, blaming myself because if I had just kept him at the church a few minutes longer or walked a different way, he might still be alive, and I feel deeply ashamed that I survived. While I don't actively try to end my life, there are nights when I drive recklessly fast down the highway and honestly don't care what happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of killing yourself or making a plan to do so, or any recent actions that put your life at risk on purpose?"

In those moments, I'm just so overwhelmed that I don't really care what happens to me.

could no longer keep functioning in his calling while feeling constantly on edge.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to love running open gym nights and playing ball with the kids, but now even when I’m there in the gym I just kind of go through the motions and can’t feel any real joy or excitement about it anymore, so I make excuses to let other leaders take over instead of me. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): When I sit down to write a sermon or even just read my Bible, my mind keeps drifting off or going blank, and I’ll catch myself staring at the same verse for ten minutes because I just can’t hold a thought long enough to finish anything. \n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere, especially if I hear a car backfire or a siren, I’m right back on that sidewalk with that boy bleeding in front of me, hearing the shots and feeling the concrete under my face like it’s happening again, and it plays over in my head on a loop no matter how much I pray or try to distract myself. \n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I plan my whole day around not having to walk past that corner or be outside when it’s getting dark, and if a church event means I’d have to be on that block or even talk about the shooting, I suddenly find a reason not to go or change the subject so I don’t have to feel everything come rushing back. \n- S073 (Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame): I keep telling myself that if I had kept him talking at the church a few more minutes or insisted on walking him a different way home, he might still be alive, and I can’t shake the feeling that I failed him as a minister and as a man. \n- S074 (Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State): Most days I carry around this heavy mix of fear and guilt—like there’s a knot in my chest—and even when I’m smiling in front of the congregation, inside I feel tense, ashamed I survived, and angry at myself for not being stronger. \n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m standing in a circle praying with folks I’ve known for years, I feel like there’s a glass wall between me and everybody else, like they’re living in a different world and I’m just pretending to be part of it. \n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): Little things set me off now—like if someone on the train bumps into me or a kid at youth group starts joking too loud—and before I know it I’m snapping, raising my voice, and then feeling awful afterward because that’s not how I’m supposed to be with people. \n- S078 (Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior): On the nights when the memories won’t let up, I’ll jump in my car and drive way too fast through the city or down the highway, almost daring something to happen because in those moments I don’t really care what happens to me. \n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Anytime I’m out in the neighborhood, especially near that block, my body tenses up automatically—I’m constantly scanning parked cars and rooftops, watching people’s hands, checking where I could duck if shots rang out, and I can’t relax enough to just walk like a normal person. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past couple of years I’ve had to back out of evening services, youth events near that block, and even some seminary deadlines because I get too keyed up or distracted to follow through, and it’s starting to strain things with the pastors who count on me and leave me feeling like I’m failing in my calling.\n- duration: These problems have been going on ever since the shooting about a little over two years ago and really haven’t let up.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: I feel like I’m always on guard and tense, like something bad is about to happen every time I’m out in the neighborhood.\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Chief Complaint, S077 (Irritability_Aggression), S079 (Hypervigilance)\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that you feel extremely on edge and irritable. Mention that you find yourself snapping at people over small things (like kids joking too loudly or people bumping into you) and that you are constantly on guard, scanning your surroundings when you are out. You can also mention that on nights when the memories won't let up, you feel highly restless."
